      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.21">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.21 THIRD-PARTY PAYMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Obligation to pay.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> relieves an insurer or similar third party from an otherwise valid obligation to pay, or changes the validity of an obligation to pay, for services rendered to a child with a disability, and the child's family. A school district shall pay the nonfederal share of medical assistance services provided according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.26">256B.0625, subdivision 26</a>. Eligible expenditures must not be made from federal funds or funds used to match other federal funds. Any federal disallowances are the responsibility of the school district. A school district may pay or reimburse co-payments, coinsurance, deductibles, and other enrollee cost-sharing amounts, on behalf of the student or family, in connection with health and related services provided under an individual educational plan or individualized family service plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Third-party reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning July 1, 2000, districts shall seek reimbursement from insurers and similar third parties for the cost of services provided by the district whenever the services provided by the district are otherwise covered by the child's health coverage. Districts shall request, but may not require, the child's family to provide information about the child's health coverage when a child with a disability begins to receive services from the district of a type that may be reimbursable, and shall request, but may not require, updated information after that as needed.</p>
          <p>(b) For children enrolled in medical assistance under chapter 256B or MinnesotaCare under chapter 256L who have no other health coverage, a district shall provide an initial and annual written notice to the enrolled child's parent or legal representative of its intent to seek reimbursement from medical assistance or MinnesotaCare for:</p>
          <p>(1) the evaluations required as part of the individualized education program process or individualized family service plan process; and</p>
          <p>(2) health-related services provided by the district according to the individualized education program or individualized family service plan.</p>
          <p class="bl">The initial notice must give the child's parent or legal representative the right to request a copy of the child's education records on the health-related services that the district provided to the child and disclosed to a third-party payer.</p>
          <p>(c) The district shall give the parent or legal representative annual written notice of:</p>
          <p>(1) the district's intent to seek reimbursement from medical assistance or MinnesotaCare for evaluations required as part of the individualized education program process or individualized family service plan process, and for health-related services provided by the district according to the individualized education program or individualized family service plan;</p>
          <p>(2) the right of the parent or legal representative to request a copy of all records concerning individualized education program or individualized family service plan health-related services disclosed by the district to any third party; and</p>
          <p>(3) the right of the parent or legal representative to withdraw consent for disclosure of a child's records at any time without consequence.</p>
          <p class="bl">The written notice shall be provided as part of the written notice required by Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 300.504 or 303.520. The district must ensure that the parent of a child with a disability is given notice, in understandable language, of federal and state procedural safeguards available to the parent under this paragraph and paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(d) In order to access the private health care coverage of a child who is covered by private health care coverage in whole or in part, a district must:</p>
          <p>(1) obtain annual written informed consent from the parent or legal representative, in compliance with subdivision 5; and</p>
          <p>(2) inform the parent or legal representative that a refusal to permit the district or state Medicaid agency to access their private health care coverage does not relieve the district of its responsibility to provide all services necessary to provide free and appropriate public education at no cost to the parent or legal representative.</p>
          <p>(e) If the commissioner of human services obtains federal approval to exempt covered individualized education program or individualized family service plan health-related services from the requirement that private health care coverage refuse payment before medical assistance may be billed, paragraphs (b), (c), and (d) shall also apply to students with a combination of private health care coverage and health care coverage through medical assistance or MinnesotaCare.</p>
          <p>(f) In the event that Congress or any federal agency or the Minnesota legislature or any state agency establishes lifetime limits, limits for any health care services, cost-sharing provisions, or otherwise provides that individualized education program or individualized family service plan health-related services impact benefits for persons enrolled in medical assistance or MinnesotaCare, the amendments to this subdivision adopted in 2002 are repealed on the effective date of any federal or state law or regulation that imposes the limits. In that event, districts must obtain informed consent consistent with this subdivision as it existed prior to the 2002 amendments and subdivision 5, before seeking reimbursement for children enrolled in medical assistance under chapter 256B or MinnesotaCare under chapter 256L who have no other health care coverage.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use of reimbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>School districts must reserve third-party revenue and must spend the reimbursements received only to:</p>
          <p>(1) retain an amount sufficient to compensate the district for its administrative costs of obtaining reimbursements;</p>
          <p>(2) regularly obtain from education- and health-related entities training and other appropriate technical assistance designed to improve the district's ability to access third-party payments for individualized education program or individualized family service plan health-related services; or</p>
          <p>(3) reallocate reimbursements for the benefit of students with individualized education programs or individualized family service plans in the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Parents not obligated to use health coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent required by federal law, a school district may not require parents of children with disabilities, if they would incur a financial cost, to use private or public health coverage to pay for the services that must be provided under an individualized education program or individualized family service plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Informed consent.</span></h2>
          <p>When obtaining informed consent, consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.05">13.05</a>, subdivision 4a; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.77#stat.256B.77.2">256B.77, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (p); and Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, parts 99, 300, and 303, to bill health plans for covered services, the school district must notify the legal representative (1) that the cost of the person's private health insurance premium may increase due to providing the covered service in the school setting, (2) that the school district may pay certain enrollee health plan costs, including but not limited to, co-payments, coinsurance, deductibles, premium increases or other enrollee cost-sharing amounts for health and related services required by an individual service plan, or individualized family service plan, and (3) that the school's billing for each type of covered service may affect service limits and prior authorization thresholds. The informed consent may be revoked in writing at any time by the person authorizing the billing of the health plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">District obligation to provide service.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent required by federal law, no school district may deny, withhold, or delay any service that must be provided under an individualized education program or individualized family service plan because a family has refused to provide informed consent to bill a health plan for services or a health plan company has refused to pay any, all, or a portion of the cost of services billed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.21.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">District disclosure of information.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district may disclose information contained in a student's individualized education program, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.32#stat.13.32.3">13.32, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), and Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, parts 99, 300, and 303; including records of the student's diagnosis and treatment, to a health plan company only with the signed and dated consent of the student's parent, or other legally authorized individual. The school district shall disclose only that information necessary for the health plan company to decide matters of coverage and payment. A health plan company may use the information only for making decisions regarding coverage and payment, and for any other use permitted by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.1.17.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/559/#laws.0.2.0">1961 c 559 s 2</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.125A.22">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.22 COMMUNITY TRANSITION INTERAGENCY COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <p>A district, group of districts, or special education cooperative, in cooperation with the county or counties in which the district or cooperative is located, may establish a community transition interagency committee for youth with disabilities, beginning at grade 9 or age equivalent, and their families. Members of the committee may include representatives from special education, vocational and regular education, community education, postsecondary education and training institutions, mental health, adults with disabilities who have received transition services if such persons are available, parents of youth with disabilities, local business or industry, rehabilitation services, county social services, health agencies, and additional public or private adult service providers as appropriate. The committee may:</p>
        <p>(1) identify current services, programs, and funding sources provided within the community for secondary and postsecondary aged youth with disabilities and their families that prepare them for further education; employment, including integrated competitive employment; and independent living;</p>
        <p>(2) facilitate the development of multiagency teams to address present and future transition needs of individual students on their individualized education programs;</p>
        <p>(3) develop a community plan to include mission, goals, and objectives, and an implementation plan to assure that transition needs of individuals with disabilities are met;</p>
        <p>(4) recommend changes or improvements in the community system of transition services; and</p>
        <p>(5) exchange agency information such as appropriate data, effectiveness studies, special projects, exemplary programs, and creative funding of programs.</p>
      </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.23">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.23 AGENCY ACCESS TO NONPUBLIC DATA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of administration must prepare a form and disseminate guidelines for state agencies, political subdivisions, and other responsible authorities to use to enable a responsible authority to allow another responsible authority access to data about a child with a disability that is classified as not public. The form and guidelines must be consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.05#stat.13.05.9">13.05, subdivision 9</a>, and federal law, and are not subject to the rulemaking requirements under chapter 14.</p>
      </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.24">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.24 PARENT ADVISORY COUNCILS.</h1>
        <p>In order to increase the involvement of parents of children with disabilities in district policy making and decision making, school districts must have a special education advisory council that is incorporated into the district's special education system plan.</p>
        <p>(1) This advisory council may be established either for individual districts or in cooperation with other districts who are members of the same special education cooperative.</p>
        <p>(2) A district may set up this council as a subgroup of an existing board, council, or committee.</p>
        <p>(3) At least half of the designated council members must be parents of students with a disability. When a nonpublic school is located in the district, the council must include at least one member who is a parent of a nonpublic school student with a disability, or an employee of a nonpublic school if no parent of a nonpublic school student with a disability is available to serve. Each local council must meet no less than once each year. The number of members, frequency of meetings, and operational procedures are to be locally determined.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.24.history
			">

      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.25">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.25 LEGISLATIVE COMMITMENT TO CONCILIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.25.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Policy statement.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that conciliation conferences pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> serve better than formal hearings to promote communications between parents and school staff and to reach prompt, shared decisions about educational programs for children with a disability. Further, the legislature urges the United States Department of Education and the United States Office of Civil Rights to acknowledge that the conciliation conference process violates no federal statute or regulation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.25.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State plan.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall not adopt any provision in the state plan for special education that reduces the opportunities for parents and school districts to resolve their differences through conciliation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.3.8.0">1981 c 358 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.60.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 60</a>,164; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">INTERAGENCY EARLY CHILDHOOD INTERVENTION SYSTEM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.259">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.259 CITATION; INTERAGENCY EARLY CHILDHOOD INTERVENTION SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.26">125A.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a> may be cited as the "Interagency Early Childhood Intervention System."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.259.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.26">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.26 PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p>It is the policy of the state to develop and implement comprehensive, coordinated, multidisciplinary interagency programs of early intervention services for children with disabilities and their families.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.27">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.27 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.259">125A.259</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coordinate.</span></h2>
          <p>"Coordinate" means to provide ready access to a community's services and resources to meet child and family needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Core early intervention services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Core early intervention services" means services that are available at no cost to children and families. These services include:</p>
          <p>(1) identification and referral;</p>
          <p>(2) screening;</p>
          <p>(3) evaluation;</p>
          <p>(4) assessment;</p>
          <p>(5) service coordination;</p>
          <p>(6) special education and related services provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.08">125A.08</a>, and United States Code, title 20, section 1401; and</p>
          <p>(7) protection of parent and child rights by means of procedural safeguards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">County board.</span></h2>
          <p>"County board" means a county board established under chapter 375.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Early intervention record.</span></h2>
          <p>"Early intervention record" means any personally identifiable information about a child or the child's family that is generated by the early intervention system, and that pertains to evaluation and assessment, development of an individualized family service plan, and the delivery of early intervention services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Early intervention services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Early intervention services" means services provided in conformity with an individualized family service plan that are designed to meet the special developmental needs of a child eligible under Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, part 303, and the needs of the child's family related to enhancing the child's development and that are selected in collaboration with the parent. These services include core early intervention services and additional early intervention services listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.29">125A.29</a> and services defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 303, et seq.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Early intervention system.</span></h2>
          <p>"Early intervention system" means the total effort in the state to meet the needs of eligible children and their families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Eligibility for Part C.</span></h2>
          <p>"Eligibility for Part C" means eligibility for infant and toddler intervention services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.02">125A.02</a> and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/3525.1350">3525.1350</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Facilitate payment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Facilitate payment" means helping families access necessary public or private assistance that provides payment for services required to meet needs identified in a service plan, individualized education program (IEP), individual service plan (ISP), or individualized family service plan (IFSP), according to time frames required by the plan. This may also include activities to collect fees for services provided on a sliding fee basis, where permitted by state law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Individualized family service plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Individualized family service plan" or "IFSP" means a written plan for providing services to a child age birth to three years and the child's family.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Interagency child find systems.</span></h2>
          <p>"Interagency child find systems" means activities developed on an interagency basis with the involvement of interagency early intervention committees and other relevant community groups, including primary referral sources included in Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/303.303">303.303 </a>(c), using rigorous standards to actively seek out, identify, and refer infants and young children, with, or at risk of, disabilities, and their families, to reduce the need for future services. The child find system must mandate referrals for a child under the age of three who: (1) is the subject of a substantiated case of abuse or neglect, or (2) is identified as directly affected by illegal substance abuse, or withdrawal symptoms resulting from prenatal drug exposure, to reduce the need for future services. The referral procedures must specify that a referral must occur within seven calendar days from the date of identification.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Local primary agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local primary agency" means the agency designated jointly by the school and county board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.29">125A.29</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Natural environments.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural environments" means the child's home and community settings in which children without disabilities participate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Parent" means "parent" as defined by Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/303.27">303.27</a>, or a surrogate parent appointed in accordance with Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/303.422">303.422</a>, or United States Code, title 20, section 1439(a)(5).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Part C state plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Part C state plan" means the annual state plan application approved by the federal government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Pay for.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pay for" means using federal, state, local, and private dollars available for early intervention services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Respite.</span></h2>
          <p>"Respite" means short-term, temporary care provided to a child with a disability due to the temporary absence or need for relief of the family member or members or primary caregiver, normally providing the care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">State lead agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"State lead agency" means the state agency receiving federal funds for the purposes of providing early intervention services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.27.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Surrogate parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Surrogate parent" means a person appointed by the local education agency to assure that the rights of the child to early intervention services are protected. A person cannot be a surrogate parent to a child for whom the person provides early intervention services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.42.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 42</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.15.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.3.10.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/294/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 294 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.13.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 13</a>-17; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.6.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.5.3.0">2024 c 109 art 5 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.28">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.28 STATE INTERAGENCY COORDINATING COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>An Interagency Coordinating Council of at least 18, but not more than 26 members is established, in compliance with Public Law 108-446, section 641. The members must be appointed by the governor and reasonably represent the population of Minnesota. Council members must elect the council chair, who may not be a representative of the Department of Children, Youth, and Families. The council must be composed of at least five parents, including persons of color, of children with disabilities under age 12, including at least three parents of a child with a disability under age seven, five representatives of public or private providers of services for children with disabilities under age five, including a special education director, county social service director, local Head Start director, and a community health services or public health nursing administrator, one member of the senate, one member of the house of representatives, one representative of teacher preparation programs in early childhood-special education or other preparation programs in early childhood intervention, at least one representative of advocacy organizations for children with disabilities under age five, one physician who cares for young children with special health care needs, one representative each from the commissioners of commerce, education, health, human services, and children, youth, and families, a representative from the state agency responsible for child care, foster care, mental health, homeless coordinator of education of homeless children and youth, and a representative from Indian health services or a tribal council. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>, subdivisions 2 to 4, apply to the council. The council must meet at least quarterly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.28.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Implementing policy; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The council must address methods of implementing the state policy of developing and implementing comprehensive, coordinated, multidisciplinary interagency programs of early intervention services for children with disabilities and their families.</p>
          <p>(b) The duties of the council include recommending policies to ensure a comprehensive and coordinated system of all state and local agency services for children under age five with disabilities and their families. The policies must address how to incorporate each agency's services into a unified state and local system of multidisciplinary assessment practices, individual intervention plans, comprehensive systems to find children in need of services, methods to improve public awareness, and assistance in determining the role of interagency early intervention committees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.28.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recommendations; report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 30 days of receiving the annual determination from the federal Office of Special Education on the Minnesota Part C Annual Performance Report, the council must recommend to the governor and the commissioners of children, youth, and families; education; health; human services; commerce; and employment and economic development policies for a comprehensive and coordinated system.</p>
          <p>(b) Annually, the council must prepare and submit a report to the governor and the secretary of the federal Department of Education on the status of early intervention services and programs for infants and toddlers with disabilities and their families under the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485 (Part C, Public Law 102-119), as operated in Minnesota. The Minnesota Part C annual performance report may serve as the report.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.28.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, the State Interagency Coordinating Council does not expire unless federal law no longer requires the existence of the council or committee.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/192/#laws.0.19.0">1997 c 192 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.43.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 43</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.16.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.51.0">2000 c 254 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/3/#laws.1.14.0">1Sp2001 c 3 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.3.9.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.18.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.3.13.0">2009 c 96 art 3 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.9.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/286/#laws.8.14.0">2014 c 286 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.5.18.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 5 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.20.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.29">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.29 RESPONSIBILITIES OF COUNTY BOARDS AND SCHOOL BOARDS.</h1>
        <p>(a) It is the joint responsibility of county boards and school boards to coordinate, provide, and pay for appropriate services, and to facilitate payment for services from public and private sources. Appropriate services for children eligible under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.02">125A.02</a> must be determined in consultation with parents, physicians, and other educational, medical, health, and human services providers. The services provided must be in conformity with:</p>
        <p>(1) an IFSP for each eligible infant and toddler from birth through age two and the infant's or toddler's family including:</p>
        <p>(i) American Indian infants and toddlers with disabilities and their families residing on a reservation geographically located in the state;</p>
        <p>(ii) infants and toddlers with disabilities who are homeless children and their families; and</p>
        <p>(iii) infants and toddlers with disabilities who are wards of the state; or</p>
        <p>(2) an individualized education program (IEP) or individual service plan (ISP) for each eligible child ages three through four.</p>
        <p>(b) Appropriate early intervention services include services provided in conformity with an IFSP that are designed to meet the special developmental needs of an eligible child and the needs of the child's family related to enhancing the child's development and that are selected in collaboration with the parent. These services include core early intervention services and additional early intervention services listed in this section and infant and toddler intervention services defined under United States Code, title 20, sections 1431 to 1444, and Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 303, including service coordination under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.33">125A.33</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) School and county boards shall coordinate early intervention services. In the absence of agreements established according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.39">125A.39</a>, service responsibilities for children birth through age two are as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) school boards must provide, pay for, and facilitate payment for special education and related services required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.06">125A.06</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) county boards must provide, pay for, and facilitate payment for noneducational services of social work, psychology, transportation and related costs, nursing, respite, and nutrition services not required under clause (1).</p>
        <p>(d) School and county boards may develop an interagency agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.39">125A.39</a> to establish agency responsibility that assures early intervention services are coordinated, provided, paid for, and that payment is facilitated from public and private sources.</p>
        <p>(e) County and school boards must jointly determine the primary agency in this cooperative effort and must notify the commissioner of the state lead agency of their decision.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.44.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 44</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.19.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.17.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 17</a>; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.10.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.30 INTERAGENCY EARLY INTERVENTION COMMITTEES.</h1>
        <p>(a) A group of school districts or special education cooperatives, in cooperation with the county and tribal health and human service agencies located in the county or counties in which the districts or cooperatives are located, must establish an Interagency Early Intervention Committee for children with disabilities under age five and their families under this section, and for children with disabilities ages three to 22 consistent with the requirements under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.023">125A.023 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.027">125A.027</a>. Committees must include representatives of local health, education, and county human service agencies, early childhood family education programs, Head Start, parents of young children with disabilities under age 12, child care resource and referral agencies, school readiness programs, current service providers, and agencies that serve families experiencing homelessness, and may also include representatives from other private or public agencies and school nurses. The committee must elect a chair from among its members and must meet at least quarterly.</p>
        <p>(b) The committee must develop and implement interagency policies and procedures concerning the following ongoing duties:</p>
        <p>(1) develop public awareness systems designed to inform potential recipient families, especially parents with premature infants, or infants with other physical risk factors associated with learning or development complications, of available programs and services;</p>
        <p>(2) to reduce families' need for future services, and especially parents with premature infants, or infants with other physical risk factors associated with learning or development complications, implement interagency child find systems designed to actively seek out, identify, and refer infants and young children with, or at risk of, disabilities, including a child under the age of three who: (i) is the subject of a substantiated case of abuse or neglect or (ii) is identified as directly affected by illegal substance abuse, or withdrawal symptoms resulting from prenatal drug exposure;</p>
        <p>(3) implement a process for assuring that services involve cooperating agencies at all steps leading to individualized programs;</p>
        <p>(4) identify the current services and funding being provided within the community for children with disabilities under age five and their families; and</p>
        <p>(5) develop a plan for the allocation and expenditure of federal early intervention funds under United States Code, title 20, section 1471 et seq. (Part C, Public Law 108-446) and United States Code, title 20, section 631, et seq. (Chapter I, Public Law 89-313).</p>
        <p>(c) The local committee shall also participate in needs assessments and program planning activities conducted by local social service, health and education agencies for young children with disabilities and their families.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.45.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 45</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.1.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/123/#laws.0.14.0">1999 c 123 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.3.12.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 3 s 12</a>; art 10 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.20.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 20</a>; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.11.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.4.9.0">2014 c 272 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/8/#laws.5.5.0">1Sp2020 c 8 art 5 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.31">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.31 LOCAL PRIMARY AGENCY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The local primary agency must:</p>
        <p>(1) facilitate the development of annual fund requests that identify arrangements with other local and regional agencies providing services as part of the state's early childhood intervention system and that result in service availability on a year-round basis, as necessary;</p>
        <p>(2) administer funds received through the annual fund request;</p>
        <p>(3) provide oversight for data collection efforts;</p>
        <p>(4) facilitate completion of interagency early intervention committee duties as indicated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.30">125A.30</a>;</p>
        <p>(5) request mediation from the state lead agency, if necessary;</p>
        <p>(6) request assistance from the state lead agency when disputes between agencies cannot be resolved within 20 calendar days; and</p>
        <p>(7) receive written requests from parents for matters that may be resolved through due process hearings.</p>
        <p>(b) When the local primary agency is not an education agency, resources distributed under the early intervention fund must be transferred from a local educational agency to a noneducation agency using a state provided contract. A local primary agency may budget for indirect costs at an amount not to exceed five percent of the amount allocated from the early intervention fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.46.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 46</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.32">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.32 INDIVIDUALIZED FAMILY SERVICE PLAN (IFSP).</h1>
        <p>(a) A team must participate in IFSP meetings to develop the IFSP. The team shall include:</p>
        <p>(1) a parent or parents of the child, as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/303.27">303.27</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) other family members, as requested by the parent, if feasible to do so;</p>
        <p>(3) an advocate or person outside of the family, if the parent requests that the person participate;</p>
        <p>(4) the service coordinator who has been working with the family since the initial referral, or who has been designated by the public agency to be responsible for implementation of the IFSP and coordination with other agencies including transition services;</p>
        <p>(5) a person or persons involved in conducting evaluations and assessments; and</p>
        <p>(6) as appropriate, persons who will be providing early intervention services under the plan to the child or family.</p>
        <p>(b) The IFSP must include:</p>
        <p>(1) information about the child's developmental status;</p>
        <p>(2) family information, with the consent of the family;</p>
        <p>(3) measurable results or major outcomes expected to be achieved by the child with the family's assistance, that include developmentally appropriate preliteracy and language skills for the child, and the criteria, procedures, and timelines;</p>
        <p>(4) specific early intervention services based on peer-reviewed research, to the extent practicable, necessary to meet the unique needs of the child and the family to achieve the outcomes;</p>
        <p>(5) payment arrangements, if any;</p>
        <p>(6) medical and other services that the child needs, but that are not required under the Individual with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, section 1471 et seq. (Part C, Public Law 108-446) including funding sources to be used in paying for those services and the steps that will be taken to secure those services through public or private sources;</p>
        <p>(7) dates and duration of early intervention services;</p>
        <p>(8) name of the service coordinator;</p>
        <p>(9) steps to be taken to support a child's transition from infant and toddler intervention services to other appropriate services, including convening a transition conference at least 90 days or, at the discretion of all parties, not more than nine months before the child is eligible for preschool services; and</p>
        <p>(10) authorized signatures of the agencies responsible for providing, paying for, or facilitating payment, or any combination of these, for infant and toddler intervention services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.47.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 47</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.21.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.12.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.33">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.33 SERVICE COORDINATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) The team responsible for the initial evaluation and the child- and family-directed assessment and for developing the IFSP under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.32">125A.32</a>, if appropriate, must select a service coordinator to carry out service coordination activities on an interagency basis. Service coordination must actively promote a family's capacity and competency to identify, obtain, coordinate, monitor, and evaluate resources and services to meet the family's needs. Service coordination activities include:</p>
        <p>(1) coordinating the performance of evaluations and assessments;</p>
        <p>(2) facilitating and participating in the development, review, and evaluation of individualized family service plans;</p>
        <p>(3) assisting families in identifying available service providers;</p>
        <p>(4) coordinating and monitoring the delivery of available services;</p>
        <p>(5) informing families of the availability of advocacy services;</p>
        <p>(6) coordinating with medical, health, and other service providers;</p>
        <p>(7) facilitating the development of a transition plan to preschool, school, or, if appropriate, to other services, at least 90 days before the time the child is no longer eligible for infant and toddler intervention services or, at the discretion of all parties, not more than nine months prior to the child's third birthday, if appropriate;</p>
        <p>(8) managing the early intervention record and submitting additional information to the local primary agency at the time of periodic review and annual evaluations; and</p>
        <p>(9) notifying a local primary agency when disputes between agencies impact service delivery required by an IFSP.</p>
        <p>(b) A service coordinator must be knowledgeable about children and families receiving services under this section, requirements of state and federal law, and services available in the interagency early childhood intervention system. The IFSP must include the name of the services coordinator from the profession most relevant to the child's or family's needs or who is otherwise qualified to carry out all applicable responsibilities under the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485 (Part C, Public Law 102-119), who will be responsible for implementing the early intervention services identified in the child's IFSP, including transition services, and coordination with other agencies and persons.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.48.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 48</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/123/#laws.0.15.0">1999 c 123 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.22.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.13.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.34">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.34 EARLY INTERVENTION RESPITE SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>The provision of respite services for an eligible child and family must be determined in the context of the IFSP development based on the individual needs of the child and family and with consideration given to the following criteria:</p>
        <p>(1) severity of the child's disability and needs;</p>
        <p>(2) potential risk of out-of-home placement for the child if respite services are not provided;</p>
        <p>(3) parental lack of access to informal support systems, including, but not limited to, extended family, supportive friends, and community supports;</p>
        <p>(4) presence of factors known to increase family stress, including, but not limited to, family size and presence of another child or family member with a disability;</p>
        <p>(5) the availability of other public services provided to the family that assist the parent or primary caretaker in obtaining relief from caretaking responsibilities; and</p>
        <p>(6) the perceived and expressed level of need for respite services by the parent.</p>
        <p>Counties are encouraged to make a variety of respite service models available, which may include in or out-of-home respite, family reimbursement programs, and parent-to-parent respite projects.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.49.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 49</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.35">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.35 EARLY INTERVENTION SERVICE DOLLARS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Lead agency; allocation of resources.</span></h2>
          <p>The state lead agency must administer the early intervention account that consists of federal allocations. The Part C state plan must state the amount of federal resources in the early intervention account available for use by local agencies. The state lead agency must enter into an agreement under which the commissioner of education must distribute the funds to the local primary agency designated by an Interagency Early Intervention Committee based on a formula that includes a December 1 count of the prior year of Part C eligible children for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) as provided in Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, part 303.430, to arrange for payment for early intervention services not elsewhere available, or to pay for services during the pendency of a conflict procedure, including mediation, complaints, due process hearings, and interagency disputes; and</p>
          <p>(2) to support interagency child find system activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Priority funding.</span></h2>
          <p>The priority purpose for this fund is subdivision 1, clause (1). The local primary agency must reallocate resources from the early intervention fund as necessary in order to meet this priority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Discretionary funding.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this subdivision limits the state lead agency's authority to allocate discretionary federal funds for any purpose consistent with the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485 (Part C, Public Law 102-119) and regulations adopted under United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.32.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.32.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.35.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">School boards.</span></h2>
          <p>School boards are not required to pay for services defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.29">125A.29, paragraph (c)</a>, clause (2).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.50.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 50</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/205/#laws.1.47.0">1999 c 205 art 1 s 47</a>,70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.14.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 14</a>,32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.21.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.36">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.36 PAYMENT FOR SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>Core early intervention services must be provided at public expense with no cost to parents. Parents must be requested to assist in the cost of additional early intervention services by using third-party payment sources. Payment structures permitted under state law must be used to pay for additional early intervention services. Parental financial responsibility must be clearly defined in the IFSP. A parent's inability to pay must not prohibit a child from receiving needed early intervention services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.51.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 51</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.15.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.37">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.37 PAYOR OF LAST RESORT.</h1>
        <p>The state lead agency must maintain a reserve account from federal sources to pay for services in dispute or to pay for early intervention services when local agencies have exhausted all other public and private funds available for Part C eligible children.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.52.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 52</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.17.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.38">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.38 MAINTENANCE OF EFFORT.</h1>
        <p>A county human services agency or county board must continue to provide services set forth in their county social service agency plan. The county human services agency or county board must serve children with disabilities under age five and their families, or as specified in the IFSP for children with disabilities, birth through age two, or the individual service plan of each child. Special instruction and related services for which a child with a disability is eligible under this section are the responsibility of the local school board. It is the joint responsibility of county boards and school boards to coordinate, provide, and pay for all appropriate services required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.29">125A.29</a> and to facilitate payment for services from public and private sources.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.53.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 53</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.39">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.39 LOCAL INTERAGENCY AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <p class="in">School boards and the county board may enter into agreements to cooperatively serve and provide funding for children with disabilities, under age five, and their families within a specified geographic area.</p>
        <p class="in">The local interagency agreement must address, at a minimum, the following issues:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) responsibilities of local agencies on local interagency early intervention committees (IEIC's), consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.38">125A.38</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) assignment of financial responsibility for early intervention services;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) methods to resolve intra-agency and interagency disputes;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) identification of current resources and recommendations about the allocation of additional state and federal early intervention funds under the auspices of United States Code, title 20, section 1471 et seq. (Part C, Public Law 108-446) and United States Code, title 20, section 631, et seq. (Chapter I, Public Law 89-313);</p>
        <p class="in">(5) data collection; and</p>
        <p class="in">(6) other components of the local early intervention system consistent with Public Law 102-119.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.10.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.40">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.40 THIRD-PARTY PAYMENT.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in this section relieves an insurer or similar third party from an otherwise valid obligation to pay, or changes the validity of an obligation to pay, for services rendered to a child with a disability, and the child's family.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.41">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.41 COORDINATING HEALTH INSURANCE BENEFITS.</h1>
        <p>The Department of Health must provide technical assistance in a timely manner to service coordinators, parents of children with disabilities, and agencies in situations requiring the coordination of health insurance benefits, or the identification of third-party payor responsibilities to provide necessary health benefits.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.54.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 54</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.42">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.42 PROCEDURAL SAFEGUARDS; PARENT AND CHILD RIGHTS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) This section applies to local school and county boards for children from birth through age two who are eligible for Part C, Public Law 108-446, and their families. This section must be consistent with the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485 (Part C, Public Law 108-446), regulations adopted under United States Code, title 20, sections 1471 to 1485, and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.259">125A.259</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A parent has the right to:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) inspect and review early intervention records;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) prior written notice of a proposed action in the parents' native language unless it is clearly not feasible to do so;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) give consent to any proposed action;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) selectively accept or decline any early intervention service; and</p>
        <p class="in">(5) resolve issues regarding the identification, evaluation, or placement of the child, or the provision of appropriate early intervention services to the child and the child's family through an impartial due process hearing pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.46">125A.46</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) The eligible child has the right to have a surrogate parent appointed by a school district as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.07">125A.07</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.11.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.43">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.43 MEDIATION PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner, or the commissioner's designee, of the state lead agency must use federal funds to provide mediation for the activities in paragraphs (b) and (c).</p>
        <p>(b) A parent may resolve a dispute regarding issues in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.42">125A.42, paragraph (b)</a>, clause (5), through mediation. If the parent chooses mediation, mediation must be voluntary on the part of the parties. The parent and the public agencies must complete the mediation process within 30 calendar days of the date the Department of Education receives a parent's written request for mediation unless a district declines mediation. The mediation process may not be used to delay a parent's right to a due process hearing. The written, signed mediation agreement is binding on both parties and is enforceable in any state court of competent jurisdiction or in a district court of the United States.</p>
        <p>(c) Resolution of a dispute through mediation, or other form of alternative dispute resolution, is not limited to formal disputes arising from the objection of a parent or guardian and is not limited to the period following a request for a due process hearing.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall provide training and resources to school districts to facilitate early identification of disputes and access to mediation.</p>
        <p>(e) The local primary agency may request mediation on behalf of involved agencies when there are disputes between agencies regarding responsibilities to coordinate, provide, pay for, or facilitate payment for early intervention services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.55.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 55</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.18.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/408/#laws.0.23.0">1998 c 408 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/294/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 294 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.16.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.44">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.44 COMPLAINT PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) An individual or organization may file a written signed complaint with the commissioner of the state lead agency alleging that one or more requirements of the Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, part 303, is not being met. The complaint must include:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) a statement that the state has violated the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, section 1471 et seq. (Part C, Public Law 108-446) or Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 303; and</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the facts on which the complaint is based.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The commissioner of the state lead agency shall receive and coordinate with other state agencies the review and resolution of a complaint within 60 calendar days according to the state interagency agreement required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>. The development and disposition of corrective action orders for nonschool agencies shall be determined by the State Agency Committee (SAC). Failure to comply with corrective orders may result in fiscal actions or other measures.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/123/#laws.0.16.0">1999 c 123 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.12.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.45">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.45 INTERAGENCY DISPUTE PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) A dispute between a school board and a county board that is responsible for implementing the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.29">125A.29</a> regarding early identification, child and family assessment, service coordination, and IFSP development and implementation must be resolved according to this subdivision when the dispute involves services provided to children and families eligible under the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, United States Code, title 20, section 1471 et seq. (Part C, Public Law 108-446).</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A dispute occurs when the school board and county board are unable to agree as to who is responsible to coordinate, provide, pay for, or facilitate payment for services from public and private sources.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Written and signed disputes must be filed with the local primary agency.</p>
        <p class="in">(d) The local primary agency must attempt to resolve the matter with the involved school board and county board and may request mediation from the commissioner of the state lead agency for this purpose.</p>
        <p class="in">(e) When interagency disputes have not been resolved within 30 calendar days, the local primary agency must request the commissioner of the state lead agency to review the matter with the commissioners of education, health, and human services and make a decision. The commissioner must provide a consistent process for reviewing those procedures. The commissioners' decision is binding subject to the right of an aggrieved party to appeal to the state court of appeals.</p>
        <p class="in">(f) The local primary agency must ensure that eligible children and their families receive early intervention services during resolution of a dispute. While a local dispute is pending, the local primary agency must either assign financial responsibility to an agency or pay for the service from the early intervention account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.35">125A.35</a>. If in resolving the dispute, it is determined that the assignment of financial responsibility was inappropriate, the responsibility for payment must be reassigned to the appropriate agency and the responsible agency must make arrangements for reimbursing any expenditures incurred by the agency originally assigned financial responsibility.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.56.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 56</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.13.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.22.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.46">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.46 DUE PROCESS HEARINGS.</h1>
        <p>The procedures for due process hearings and appeals must be the same as those in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.091">125A.091</a>. The responsibility for payment of costs and conducting due process hearings and appeals shall be allocated to the appropriate agency in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.30">125A.30</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.39">125A.39</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.42">125A.42</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
