        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.57.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 57</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.5.12.0">2004 c 294 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.125A.47"><b>125A.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.3.21.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 3 s 21</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.48">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.48 STATE INTERAGENCY AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioners of children, youth, and families; education; health; and human services must enter into an agreement to implement this section and Part C, Public Law 108-446, and as required by Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 303.523, to promote the development and implementation of interagency, coordinated, multidisciplinary state and local early childhood intervention service systems for serving eligible young children with disabilities, birth through age two, and their families and to ensure the meaningful involvement of underserved groups, including children with disabilities from minority, low-income, homeless, and rural families, and children with disabilities who are wards of the state. The agreement must be reviewed annually.</p>
        <p>(b) The state interagency agreement must outline at a minimum the conditions, procedures, purposes, and responsibilities of the participating state and local agencies for the following:</p>
        <p>(1) membership, roles, and responsibilities of a state interagency committee for the oversight of priorities and budget allocations under Part C, Public Law 108-446, and other state allocations for this program;</p>
        <p>(2) child find;</p>
        <p>(3) establishment of local interagency agreements;</p>
        <p>(4) review by a state interagency committee of the allocation of additional state and federal early intervention funds by local agencies;</p>
        <p>(5) fiscal responsibilities of the state and local agencies;</p>
        <p>(6) intra-agency and interagency dispute resolution;</p>
        <p>(7) payor of last resort;</p>
        <p>(8) maintenance of effort;</p>
        <p>(9) procedural safeguards, including mediation;</p>
        <p>(10) complaint resolution;</p>
        <p>(11) quality assurance;</p>
        <p>(12) data collection;</p>
        <p>(13) an annual summary to the state Interagency Coordinating Council regarding conflict resolution activities including disputes, due process hearings, and complaints; and</p>
        <p>(14) other components of the state and local early intervention system consistent with Public Law 108-446.</p>
        <p class="bl">Written materials must be developed for parents, IEIC's, and local service providers that describe procedures developed under this section as required by Code of Federal Regulations, title 34, section 303.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 9</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.59.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 59</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.23.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.23.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ALTERNATIVES FOR DELIVERING SPECIALIZED INSTRUCTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.50">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.50 ALTERNATIVE DELIVERY OF SPECIALIZED INSTRUCTIONAL SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner approval.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may approve applications from districts initiating or significantly changing a program to provide prevention services as an alternative to special education and other compensatory programs. A district with an approved program may provide instruction and services in a regular education classroom, or an area learning center, to eligible pupils. Pupils eligible to participate in the program are pupils who need additional academic or behavioral support to succeed in the general education environment and who may eventually qualify for special education instruction or related services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> if the intervention services authorized by this section were unavailable. A pupil with a disability as defined under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a>, whose individualized education program team has determined that the pupil does not require special education services in the area of the district's approved program, may participate in the approved program as long as participation does not result in an increase in costs for the program or displace a pupil who does not currently have a disability. Pupils may be provided services during extended school days and throughout the entire year and through the assurance of mastery program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application contents.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The application must set forth:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) instructional services available to eligible pupils under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.66#stat.124D.66.2">124D.66, subdivision 2</a>, and pupils with a disability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.02">125A.02</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) criteria to select pupils for the program and the assessment procedures to determine eligibility;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) involvement in the program of parents of pupils in the program, parent advocates, and community special education advocates;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) accounting procedures to document that federal special education money is used to supplement or increase the level of special education instruction and related services provided with state and local revenue, but in no case to supplant the state and local revenue, and that districts are expending at least the amount for special education instruction and related services required by federal law;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the role of general and special education teachers in planning, implementing, and evaluating the program;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) an annual budget detailing program expenditures; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) other information requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The application must also set forth the review and evaluation procedures to be used by the district addressing at least the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the number of pupils with and without a disability served;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the impact of the program on the academic and behavioral progress of the pupils;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the level of satisfaction teachers, parents, and pupils have with the program;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the effect of the program on the number of referrals for special education, federal Title 1, and other programs; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) cost implications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Budget review and approval.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Each year before a district receives aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.78">125A.78</a>, the district must submit to the commissioner for review and approval a budget detailing program expenditures for the fiscal year. The commissioner must determine whether the personnel, equipment, supplies, and extended school year are necessary to meet the district's obligation to provide special instruction and services to children with a disability according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a>. The commissioner may not approve revenue for any expenditures determined to be unnecessary.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner must not approve budget increases under this section that would cause the state to fail to meet maintenance of effort requirements under federal special education law. The commissioner must establish criteria for prioritizing and approving budget increases, which may include criteria such as maintaining current programs, locating programs throughout the state, and developing innovative programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Each year the district must submit to the commissioner a report containing the information described in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.50.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Pupil rights.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A pupil participating in the program must be individually evaluated according to the pupil's actual abilities and needs. A pupil who is eligible for services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> is entitled to procedural protections provided under United States Code, title 20, section 33, in any matter that affects the identification, evaluation, placement, or change in placement of a pupil. The district must ensure the protection of a pupil's civil rights, provide equal educational opportunities, and prohibit discrimination. Failure to comply with this subdivision will at least cause a district to become ineligible to participate in the program. Notwithstanding rules of the Department of Education, the commissioner cannot waive a pupil's rights under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.3.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 3</a>,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.61.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 61</a>-64,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.19.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 19</a>,20; art 5 s 55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.16.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.2.30.0">2007 c 146 art 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/8/#laws.4.2.0">1Sp2020 c 8 art 4 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.51">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.51 PLACEMENT OF CHILDREN WITHOUT DISABILITIES; EDUCATION AND TRANSPORTATION.</h1>
        <p>The responsibility for providing instruction and transportation for a pupil without a disability who has a short-term or temporary physical or emotional illness or disability, as determined by the standards of the commissioner, and who is temporarily placed for care and treatment for that illness or disability, must be determined as provided in this section.</p>
        <p>(a) The school district of residence of the pupil is the district in which the pupil's parent or guardian resides. If there is a dispute between school districts regarding residency, the district of residence is the district designated by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(b) When parental rights have been terminated by court order, the legal residence of a child placed in a residential or foster facility for care and treatment is the district in which the child resides.</p>
        <p>(c) Before the placement of a pupil for care and treatment, the district of residence must be notified and provided an opportunity to participate in the placement decision. When an immediate emergency placement is necessary and time does not permit resident district participation in the placement decision, the district in which the pupil is temporarily placed, if different from the district of residence, must notify the district of residence of the emergency placement within 15 days of the placement. When a nonresident district makes an emergency placement without first consulting with the resident district, the resident district has up to five business days after receiving notice of the emergency placement to request an opportunity to participate in the placement decision, which the placing district must then provide.</p>
        <p>(d) When a pupil without a disability is temporarily placed for care and treatment in a day program and the pupil continues to live within the district of residence during the care and treatment, the district of residence must provide instruction and necessary transportation to and from the care and treatment program for the pupil. The resident district may establish reasonable restrictions on transportation, except if a Minnesota court or agency orders the child placed at a day care and treatment program and the resident district receives a copy of the order, then the resident district must provide transportation to and from the program unless the court or agency orders otherwise. Transportation shall only be provided by the resident district during regular operating hours of the resident district. The resident district may provide the instruction at a school within the district of residence; at the pupil's residence; through an authorized online learning program provided by the pupil's resident district, district of open enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, or charter school of enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E.11">124E.11</a> if the child, or the child's parent or guardian for a pupil under the age of 18, agrees to that form of instruction; or, in the case of a placement outside of the resident district, in the district in which the day treatment program is located by paying tuition to that district. The district of placement may contract with a facility to provide instruction by teachers licensed by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board.</p>
        <p>(e) When a pupil without a disability is temporarily placed in a residential program for care and treatment, the district in which the pupil is placed must provide instruction for the pupil and necessary transportation while the pupil is receiving instruction, and in the case of a placement outside of the district of residence, the nonresident district must bill the district of residence for the actual cost of providing the instruction for the regular school year and for summer school, excluding transportation costs. If a pupil's district of residence, district of open enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, or charter school of enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E.11">124E.11</a> is authorized to provide online learning instruction under state statutes, the district in which the pupil is placed may utilize that online learning program in fulfilling its responsibility to provide instruction under this section if the child, or the child's parent or guardian for a pupil under the age of 18, agrees to that form of instruction.</p>
        <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraph (e), if the pupil is homeless and placed in a public or private homeless shelter, then the district that enrolls the pupil under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.20#stat.120A.20.2">120A.20, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b), shall provide the transportation, unless the district that enrolls the pupil and the district in which the pupil is temporarily placed agree that the district in which the pupil is temporarily placed shall provide transportation. When a pupil without a disability is temporarily placed in a residential program outside the district of residence, the administrator of the court placing the pupil must send timely written notice of the placement to the district of residence. The district of placement may contract with a residential facility to provide instruction by teachers licensed by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board. For purposes of this section, the state correctional facilities operated on a fee-for-service basis are considered to be residential programs for care and treatment.</p>
        <p>(g) The district of residence must include the pupil in its residence count of pupil units and pay tuition as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.488">123A.488</a> to the district providing the instruction. Transportation costs must be paid by the district providing the transportation and the state must pay transportation aid to that district. For purposes of computing state transportation aid, pupils governed by this subdivision must be included in the disabled transportation category if the pupils cannot be transported on a regular school bus route without special accommodations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.1.1.0">1982 c 548 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/486/#laws.0.6.0">1988 c 486 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.4.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 4</a>,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.3.8.0">1992 c 499 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.3.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.65.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 65</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.21.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 21</a>; art 5 s 55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.18.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.3.10.0">2000 c 489 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.1.13.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.3.14.0">2009 c 96 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.3.8.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.46.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.22.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.7.10.0">2023 c 55 art 7 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.26.0">2024 c 85 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.515">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.515 PLACEMENT OF STUDENTS; APPROVAL OF EDUCATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Approval of on-site education programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall approve on-site education programs for placement of children and youth in residential facilities including detention centers, before being licensed by the Department of Human Services or the Department of Corrections. Education programs in these facilities shall conform to state and federal education laws including the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act (IDEA). This section applies only to placements in children's residential facilities licensed by the Department of Human Services or the Department of Corrections. For purposes of this section, "on-site education program" means the educational services provided directly on the grounds of the children's residential facility to children and youth placed for care and treatment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.3.17.0">2006 c 263 art 3 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Responsibilities for providing education.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The district in which the children's residential facility is located must provide education services, including special education if eligible, to all students placed in a facility. If a child's district of residence, district of open enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, or charter school of enrollment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E.11">124E.11 </a>is authorized to provide online learning instruction under state statutes, the district in which the children's residential facility is located may utilize that online learning program in fulfilling its education services responsibility under this section if the child, or the child's parent or guardian for a pupil under the age of 18, agrees to that form of instruction.</p>
          <p>(b) For education programs operated by the Department of Corrections, the providing district shall be the Department of Corrections. For students remanded to the commissioner of corrections, the providing and resident district shall be the Department of Corrections.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Students without a disability from other states.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district is not required to provide education services under this section to a student who:</p>
          <p>(1) is not a resident of Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) does not have an individualized education program; and</p>
          <p>(3) does not have a tuition arrangement or agreement to pay the cost of education from the placing authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Education services required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Education services must be provided to a student beginning within three business days after the student enters the children's residential facility. The first four days of the student's placement may be used to screen the student for educational and safety issues.</p>
          <p>(b) If the student does not meet the eligibility criteria for special education, regular education services must be provided to that student.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Education programs for students placed in children's residential facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a student is placed in a children's residential facility under this section that has an on-site education program, the providing district, upon notice from the children's residential facility, must contact the resident district within one business day to determine if a student has been identified as having a disability, and to request at least the student's transcript, and for students with disabilities, the most recent individualized education program (IEP) and evaluation report. The resident district must send a facsimile copy to the providing district within two business days of receiving the request.</p>
          <p>(b) If a student placed under this section has been identified as having a disability and has an individualized education program in the resident district:</p>
          <p>(1) the providing agency must conduct an individualized education program meeting to reach an agreement about continuing or modifying special education services in accordance with the current individualized education program goals and objectives and to determine if additional evaluations are necessary; and</p>
          <p>(2) at least the following people shall receive written notice or documented phone call to be followed with written notice to attend the individualized education program meeting:</p>
          <p>(i) the person or agency placing the student;</p>
          <p>(ii) the resident district;</p>
          <p>(iii) the appropriate teachers and related services staff from the providing district;</p>
          <p>(iv) appropriate staff from the children's residential facility;</p>
          <p>(v) the parents or legal guardians of the student; and</p>
          <p>(vi) when appropriate, the student.</p>
          <p>(c) For a student who has not been identified as a student with a disability, a screening must be conducted by the providing districts as soon as possible to determine the student's educational and behavioral needs and must include a review of the student's educational records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exit report summarizing educational progress.</span></h2>
          <p>If a student has been placed in a facility under this section for 15 or more business days, the providing district must prepare an exit report summarizing the regular education, special education, evaluation, educational progress, and service information and must send the report to the resident district and the next providing district if different, the parent or legal guardian, and any appropriate social service agency. For students with disabilities, this report must include the student's IEP.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Minimum educational services required.</span></h2>
          <p>When a student is placed in a children's residential facility under this section, at a minimum, the providing district is responsible for:</p>
          <p>(1) the education necessary, including summer school services, for a student who is not performing at grade level as indicated in the education record or IEP; and</p>
          <p>(2) a school day, of the same length as the school day of the providing district, unless the unique needs of the student, as documented through the IEP or education record in consultation with treatment providers, requires an alteration in the length of the school day.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Placement, services, and due process.</span></h2>
          <p>When a student's treatment and educational needs allow, education shall be provided in a regular educational setting. The determination of the amount and site of integrated services must be a joint decision between the student's parents or legal guardians and the treatment and education staff. When applicable, educational placement decisions must be made by the IEP team of the providing district. Educational services shall be provided in conformance with the least restrictive environment principle of the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act. The providing district and children's residential facility shall cooperatively develop discipline and behavior management procedures to be used in emergency situations that comply with the Minnesota Pupil Fair Dismissal Act and other relevant state and federal laws and regulations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement for education services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Education services provided to students who have been placed under this section are reimbursable in accordance with special education and general education statutes.</p>
          <p>(b) Indirect or consultative services provided in conjunction with regular education prereferral interventions and assessment provided to regular education students suspected of being disabled and who have demonstrated learning or behavioral problems in a screening are reimbursable with special education categorical aids.</p>
          <p>(c) Regular education, including screening, provided to students with or without disabilities is not reimbursable with special education categorical aids.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.515.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Students unable to attend school but not covered under this section.</span></h2>
          <p>Students who are absent from, or predicted to be absent from, school for 15 consecutive or intermittent days, and placed at home or in facilities not licensed by the Departments of Corrections or Human Services are entitled to regular and special education services consistent with this section or Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/3525.2325">3525.2325</a>. These students include students with and without disabilities who are home due to accident or illness, in a hospital or other medical facility, or in a day treatment center.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.515.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.19.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/375/#laws.3.2.0">2002 c 375 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.3.2.0">2006 c 263 art 3 s 2</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.3.9.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 3 s 9,12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.3.3.0">2012 c 239 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.4.5.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.7.11.0">2023 c 55 art 7 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.27.0">2024 c 85 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.52">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.52 RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT FACILITIES; DEPARTMENTS OF HUMAN SERVICES AND CORRECTIONS EDUCATION SCREENING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.52.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Educational screening.</span></h2>
          <p>Secure and nonsecure residential treatment facilities licensed by the Department of Human Services or the Department of Corrections must screen each juvenile who is held in a facility for at least 72 hours, excluding weekends or holidays, using an educational screening tool identified by the department, unless the facility determines that the juvenile has a current individualized education program and obtains a copy of it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.52.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may, in consultation with the commissioners of corrections and human services, make or amend rules relating to education programs in residential treatment facilities, if necessary, to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/226/#laws.3.4.0">1995 c 226 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.66.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 66</a>,164; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/123/#laws.0.17.0">1999 c 123 s 17</a>; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.53">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.53 DIRECTOR OF A SPECIAL EDUCATION COOPERATIVE.</h1>
        <p>The authority for the selection and employment of the director of a special education cooperative established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> is vested in the governing board of the cooperative. Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, no individual shall have a right to employment as a director based on seniority or order of employment by the cooperative.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.7.5.0">1983 c 314 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.67.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 67</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.125A.54"><b>125A.54</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.3.13.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 3 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.55">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.55 ACCOMMODATING STUDENTS WITH DISABILITIES.</h1>
        <p>A school or district must provide a student who is an "individual with a disability" under Section 504 of the Rehabilitation Act of 1973, United States Code, title 29, section 794, or under the Americans with Disabilities Act, Public Law 101-336, with reasonable accommodations or modifications in programs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.10.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.69.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 69</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.56">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.56 ALTERNATE INSTRUCTION REQUIRED BEFORE ASSESSMENT REFERRAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.56.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Before a pupil in kindergarten through grade 12 is referred for a special education evaluation, the district must conduct and document at least two instructional strategies, alternatives, or interventions using a system of scientific, research-based instruction and intervention in academics or behavior, based on the pupil's needs, while the pupil is in the regular classroom. The pupil's teacher must document the results. A special education evaluation team may waive this requirement when it determines the pupil's need for the evaluation is urgent. This section may not be used to deny a pupil's right to a special education evaluation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A school district shall use alternative intervention services, including the assurance of mastery program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.66">124D.66</a>, or an early intervening services program under subdivision 2 to serve at-risk pupils who demonstrate a need for alternative instructional strategies or interventions.</p>
          <p>(c) A student identified as being unable to read at grade level under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.12">120B.12</a>, subdivision 2, paragraph (a), must be provided with alternate instruction under this subdivision that is multisensory, systematic, sequential, cumulative, and explicit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.56.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Early intervening services program.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A district may meet the requirement under subdivision 1 by establishing an early intervening services program that includes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a system of valid and reliable general outcome measures aligned to state academic standards that is administered at least three times per year to pupils in kindergarten through grade 8 who need additional academic or behavioral support to succeed in the general education environment. The school must provide interim assessments that measure pupils' performance three times per year and implement progress monitoring appropriate to the pupil. For purposes of this section, "progress monitoring" means the frequent and continuous measurement of a pupil's performance that includes these three interim assessments and other pupil assessments during the school year. A school, at its discretion, may allow pupils in grades 9 through 12 to participate in interim assessments;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a system of scientific, research-based instruction and intervention; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) an organizational plan that allows teachers, paraprofessionals, and volunteers funded through various sources to work as a grade-level team or use another configuration across grades and settings to deliver instruction. The team must be trained in scientific, research-based instruction and intervention. Teachers and paraprofessionals at a site operating under this paragraph must work collaboratively with those pupils who need additional academic or behavioral support to succeed in a general education environment.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) As an intervention under paragraph (a), clause (2), staff generating special education aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a> may provide small group instruction to pupils who need additional academic or behavioral support to succeed in the general education environment. Small group instruction that includes pupils with a disability may be provided in the general education environment if the needs of the pupils with a disability are met, consistent with their individualized education programs, and all pupils in the group receive the same level of instruction and make the same progress in the instruction or intervention. Teachers and paraprofessionals must ensure that the needs of pupils with a disability participating in small group instruction under this paragraph remain the focus of the instruction. Expenditures attributable to the time special education staff spends providing instruction to nondisabled pupils in this circumstance is eligible for special education aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a> as an incidental benefit if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the group consists primarily of disabled pupils;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) no special education staff are added to meet nondisabled pupils' needs; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the primary purpose of the instruction is to implement the individualized education programs of pupils with a disability in this group.</p>
          <p class="in">Expenditures attributable to the time special education staff spends providing small group instruction to nondisabled pupils that affords more than an incidental benefit to such pupils is not eligible for special education aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a>, except that such expenditures may be included in the alternative delivery initial aid adjustment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.78">125A.78</a> if the district has an approved program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.50">125A.50</a>. During each 60-day period that a nondisabled pupil participates in small group instruction under this paragraph, the pupil's progress monitoring data must be examined to determine whether the pupil is making progress and, if the pupil is not making progress, the pupil's intervention strategies must be changed or the pupil must be referred for a special education evaluation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.3.21.0">1989 c 329 art 3 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.38.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.2.31.0">2007 c 146 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.18.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 18</a>; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.38.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.43.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.5.4.0">2024 c 109 art 5 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ASSISTIVE TECHNOLOGY DEVICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.57">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.57 DEFINITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.57">125A.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.60">125A.60</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Assistive technology device.</span></h2>
          <p>"Assistive technology device" means any item, piece of equipment, or product system, whether acquired commercially off the shelf, modified, or customized, that is used to increase, maintain, or improve functional capabilities of a child with a disability. It does not mean a medical device that is surgically implanted or a replacement of such a device.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.57.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.6.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.3.15.0">2009 c 96 art 3 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.58">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.58 PURCHASING GUIDELINES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rights of districts to purchase school-owned assistive technology.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a child with a disability exits a district and enters a new district, the child's new district may purchase any assistive technology devices that the child's former district has purchased on the child's behalf. The child's new district must notify, in writing, the child's former district of the intent to purchase the device. The child's new district must complete a purchase agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.36">125A.36</a>. The child's former district must respond, in writing, to the request to purchase within 30 days.</p>
          <p>(b) Districts may decline to sell a device if they can demonstrate the technology is a general use device or can be modified for use by other students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Liability for used equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>The child's former district is not liable for any nonconformities in the equipment after it is purchased by the child's new district, or for injuries arising out of the use of the assistive technology device. This section does not foreclose the child's right to bring suit against the manufacturer, assistive device lessor, or assistive device dealer for nonconformities in or injuries arising out of the use of the assistive technology device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Third-party payors.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing contained in this section may be construed as decreasing the obligation of an insurance company or other third-party payor to provide coverage for assistive technology.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.58.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.7.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.70.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 70</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.59">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.59 INTERAGENCY AGREEMENT TO PURCHASE USED ASSISTIVE TECHNOLOGY DEVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.59.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Option to purchase by Department of Employment and Economic Development.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a child with a disability transitions into a work environment or enrolls in a postsecondary course or program, the Department of Employment and Economic Development may purchase any assistive technology device that the child's former district purchased on the child's behalf.</p>
          <p>(b) The Department of Employment and Economic Development may purchase an assistive technology device initially purchased by a district for a child who is currently a recipient of rehabilitation services and who needs the identical assistive technology device as stated on the recipient's individual written rehabilitation plan. The purchase may be made not more than three months before the child exits the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.59.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Liability for used equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Employment and Economic Development and the Department of Education are not liable for any nonconformities in the equipment after it is purchased by the Department of Employment and Economic Development, or for injuries arising out of the use of the assistive technology device. This section does not foreclose the child's right to bring suit against the manufacturer, assistive device lessor, or assistive device dealer for nonconformities in or injuries arising out of the use of the assistive technology device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.59.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Third-party payor.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing contained in this section may be construed as decreasing the obligation of an insurance company or other third-party payor to provide coverage for assistive technology.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.59.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.8.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.71.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 71</a>,164; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.60">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.60 PURCHASE AGREEMENT; PRICE FORMULA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner must develop guidelines for the sale of used assistive technology including a purchase agreement, a formula for establishing the sale price, and other terms and conditions of the sale.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.9.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.72.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 72</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ACADEMIES FOR THE DEAF AND BLIND</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.61">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.61 LOCATION AND STATUS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.61.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State schools at Faribault.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota State Academy for the Deaf and the Minnesota State Academy for the Blind are residential schools in Faribault. They are public schools under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15">122A.15 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.16">122A.16</a> and state educational institutions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.61.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Resource center.</span></h2>
          <p>The state academies are a resource center for school districts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.61.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/271/#laws.0.66.0">1976 c 271 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/240/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 240 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.3.27.0">1987 c 398 art 3 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/220/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 220 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.55.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.62">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.62 DUTIES OF THE BOARD OF THE MINNESOTA STATE ACADEMIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Governance.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of the Minnesota State Academies shall govern the State Academy for the Deaf and the State Academy for the Blind. The board must promote academic standards based on high expectation and an assessment system to measure academic performance toward the achievement of those standards. The board must focus on the academies' needs as a whole and not prefer one school over the other. The board of the Minnesota State Academies shall consist of nine persons. The members of the board shall be appointed by the governor with the advice and consent of the senate. One member must be from the seven-county metropolitan area, one member must be from greater Minnesota, and one member may be appointed at-large. The board must be composed of:</p>
          <p>(1) one present or former superintendent of an independent school district;</p>
          <p>(2) one present or former special education director;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner of education or the commissioner's designee;</p>
          <p>(4) one member of the blind community;</p>
          <p>(5) one member of the deaf community;</p>
          <p>(6) two members of the general public with business, administrative, or financial expertise;</p>
          <p>(7) one nonvoting, unpaid ex officio member appointed by the site council for the State Academy for the Deaf; and</p>
          <p>(8) one nonvoting, unpaid ex officio member appointed by the site council for the State Academy for the Blind.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Terms; compensation; and other.</span></h2>
          <p>The membership terms, compensation, removal of members, and filling of vacancies shall be as provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>, a member may serve not more than two consecutive four-year terms.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>All meetings of the board shall be as provided in chapter 13D and must be held in Faribault.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Most beneficial, least restrictive.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must do what is necessary to provide the most beneficial and least restrictive program of education for each pupil at the academies who is disabled by visual disability or deafness.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Planning, evaluation, and reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent required in school districts, the board must establish a process for the academies to include parent and community input in the planning, evaluation, and reporting of curriculum and pupil achievement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Site councils.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must establish, and appoint members to, a site council at each academy. The site councils shall exercise power and authority granted by the board. The board must appoint to each site council the exclusive representative's employee designee from each exclusive representative at the academies. The site councils may make a recommendation to the governor regarding board appointments no more than 30 days after receiving the list of applicants from the governor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Trustee of academies' property.</span></h2>
          <p>The board is the trustee of the academies' property. Securities and money, including income from the property, must be deposited in the state treasury according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.275">16A.275</a>. The deposits are subject to the order of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.62.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Grants and gifts.</span></h2>
          <p>The board, through the chief administrators of the academies, may apply for all competitive grants administered by agencies of the state and other government or nongovernment sources. Application may not be made for grants over which the board has discretion. Any funds received under this subdivision are appropriated and dedicated for the purpose for which they are granted. The board must annually by February 1 report to the education policy and finance committees of the legislature the amount of money it received under this subdivision and the purpose for which it was granted.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.62.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.27.0">1976 c 222 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/271/#laws.0.67.0">1976 c 271 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.3.10.0">1977 c 447 art 3 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/449/#laws.0.13.0">1977 c 449 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.95.0">1978 c 764 s 95</a>,96; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.3.17.0">1979 c 334 art 3 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/560/#laws.0.46.0">1982 c 560 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/240/#laws.0.3.0">1985 c 240 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.1.55.0">1987 c 384 art 1 s 55</a>; art 2 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.3.28.0">1987 c 398 art 3 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/220/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 220 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.11.11.0">1991 c 265 art 11 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.11.5.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 11 s 5</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.10.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.14.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 14</a>-21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.20.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.7.4.0">2006 c 263 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.7.1.0">2009 c 96 art 7 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.63">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.63 RESOURCES; DEAF OR HARD-OF-HEARING AND BLIND OR VISUALLY IMPAIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.63.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.5.31.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 5 s 31</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.63.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The department must offer summer institutes or other training programs throughout the state for deaf or hard-of-hearing, blind or visually impaired, and multiply disabled pupils. The department must also offer workshops for teachers, and leadership development for teachers.</p>
          <p>(b) Training and workshop programs offered under paragraph (a) must help promote and develop education programs offered by school districts or other organizations. The programs must assist school districts or other organizations to develop innovative programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.63.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Programs by nonprofits.</span></h2>
          <p>The department may contract to have nonprofit organizations provide programs under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.63.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Advisory committees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish advisory committees for the deaf and hard-of-hearing and for the blind and visually impaired. The advisory committees shall develop recommendations and submit an annual report to the commissioner on the form and in the manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The advisory committees for the deaf and hard-of-hearing and for the blind and visually impaired shall meet periodically at least four times per year. The committees must each review, approve, and submit a biennial report to the commissioner, the education policy and finance committees of the legislature, and the Commission of the Deaf, DeafBlind and Hard of Hearing. The reports must, at least:</p>
          <p>(1) identify and report the aggregate, data-based education outcomes for children with the primary disability classification of deaf and hard-of-hearing or of blind and visually impaired, consistent with the commissioner's child count reporting practices, the commissioner's state and local outcome data reporting system by district and region, and the school performance report cards under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.36#stat.120B.36.1">120B.36, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) describe the implementation of a data-based plan for improving the education outcomes of deaf and hard-of-hearing or blind and visually impaired children that is premised on evidence-based best practices, and provide a cost estimate for ongoing implementation of the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.63.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Statewide hearing loss early education intervention coordinator.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The coordinator shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) collaborate with the early hearing detection and intervention coordinator for the Department of Health, deaf and hard-of-hearing state specialist, and the Department of Health Early Hearing Detection and Intervention Advisory Council;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) coordinate and support Department of Education and Department of Children, Youth, and Families early hearing detection and intervention teams;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) leverage resources by serving as a liaison between interagency early intervention committees; part C coordinators from the Departments of Education, Health, and Human Services; Department of Education regional low-incidence facilitators; service coordinators from school districts; Minnesota children with special health needs in the Department of Health; public health nurses; child find; Department of Human Services Deaf and Hard-of-Hearing Services Division; and others as appropriate;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) identify, support, and promote culturally appropriate and evidence-based early intervention practices for infants with hearing loss, and provide training, outreach, and use of technology to increase consistency in statewide service provision;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) identify culturally appropriate specialized reliable and valid instruments to assess and track the progress of children with hearing loss and promote their use;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) ensure that early childhood providers, parents, and members of the individual family service and intervention plan are provided with child progress data resulting from specialized assessments;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) educate early childhood providers and teachers of the deaf and hard-of-hearing to use developmental data from specialized assessments to plan and adjust individual family service plans; and</p>
          <p class="in">(8) make recommendations that would improve educational outcomes to the early hearing detection and intervention committee, the commissioners of education; children, youth, and families; and health, the Commission of the Deaf, DeafBlind and Hard of Hearing, and the advisory council for the deaf and hard-of-hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The Department of Education and Department of Children, Youth, and Families must provide aggregate data regarding outcomes of deaf and hard-of-hearing children who receive early intervention services within the state in accordance with the state performance plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.3.30.0">1987 c 398 art 3 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/220/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 220 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.11.9.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 11 s 9</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.3.9.0">2006 c 263 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.7.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.15.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.3.16.0">2009 c 96 art 3 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.5.19.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 5 s 19</a>-22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.29.11.0">2016 c 189 art 29 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/121/#laws.0.7.0">2018 c 121 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.1.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.64">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.64 POWERS OF BOARD OF THE MINNESOTA STATE ACADEMIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personnel.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of the Minnesota State Academies may employ central administrative staff members and other personnel necessary to provide and support programs and services at each academy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Department assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of the Minnesota State Academies may require the Department of Education to provide program leadership, program monitoring, and technical assistance at the academies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Unclassified positions.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of the Minnesota State Academies may place any position other than residential academies administrator in the unclassified service. The position must meet the criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.08#stat.43A.08.1a">43A.08, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Residential and building maintenance services.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of the Minnesota State Academies may enter into agreements with public or private agencies or institutions to provide residential and building maintenance services. The board of the Minnesota State Academies must first decide that contracting for the services is more efficient and less expensive than not contracting for them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Student teachers and professional trainees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board of the Minnesota State Academies may enter into agreements with teacher preparation institutions for student teachers to get practical experience at the academies. A licensed teacher must provide appropriate supervision of each student teacher.</p>
          <p>(b) The board of the Minnesota State Academies may enter into agreements with accredited higher education institutions for certain student trainees to get practical experience at the academies. The students must be preparing themselves in a professional field that provides special services to children with a disability in school programs. To be a student trainee in a field, a person must have completed at least two years of an approved program in the field. A person who is licensed or registered in the field must provide appropriate supervision of each student trainee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.64.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exemption to September 1 school start restriction.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.40">120A.40</a>, the board of the Minnesota State Academies for the Deaf and Blind may begin the school year any day prior to September 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.125A.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/220/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 220 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.11.10.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 11 s 10</a>,11; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.22.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.21.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 21</a>; art 10 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.30.0">2010 c 382 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.125A.65">
        <h1 class="shn">125A.65 ATTENDANCE AT ACADEMIES FOR THE DEAF AND BLIND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Responsibility allocated.</span></h2>
          <p>Responsibility for special instruction and services for a blind/visually impaired or deaf/hard-of-hearing child attending the Minnesota State Academy for the Deaf or the Minnesota State Academy for the Blind must be determined in subdivisions 2 to 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Child's legal residence.</span></h2>
          <p>The legal residence of the child is the district in which the child's parent or guardian resides.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Educational program; tuition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When it is determined pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.69#stat.125A.69.1">125A.69, subdivision 1</a> or 2, that the child is entitled to attend either school, the board of the Minnesota State Academies must provide the appropriate educational program for the child.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal year 2006, the board of the Minnesota State Academies must make a tuition charge to the child's district of residence for the cost of providing the program. The amount of tuition charged must not exceed the sum of (1) the general education revenue formula allowance times the pupil unit weighting factor pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a> for that child, for the amount of time the child is in the program, plus (2), if the child was enrolled at the Minnesota State Academies on October 1 of the previous fiscal year, the compensatory education revenue attributable to that child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10">126C.10</a>, subdivision 3. The district of the child's residence must pay the tuition and may claim general education aid for the child. Tuition received by the board of the Minnesota State Academies, except for tuition for compensatory education revenue under this paragraph and tuition received under subdivision 4, must be deposited in the state treasury as provided in subdivision 8.</p>
          <p>(c) For fiscal year 2007 and later, the district of the child's residence shall claim general education revenue for the child, except as provided in this paragraph. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.47">127A.47</a>, subdivision 1, an amount equal to the general education revenue formula allowance times the pupil unit weighting factor pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a> for that child for the amount of time the child is in the program, as adjusted according to subdivision 8, paragraph (d), must be paid to the Minnesota State Academies. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.15">126C.15</a>, subdivision 2, paragraph (d), the compensatory education revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10">126C.10</a>, subdivision 3, attributable to children enrolled at the Minnesota State Academies on October 1 of the previous fiscal year must be paid to the Minnesota State Academies. General education aid paid to the Minnesota State Academies under this paragraph must be credited to their general operation account. Other general education aid attributable to the child must be paid to the district of the child's residence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Unreimbursed costs.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For fiscal year 2006, in addition to the tuition charge allowed in subdivision 3, the academies may charge the child's district of residence for the academy's unreimbursed cost of providing an instructional aide assigned to that child, after deducting the special education aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a>, attributable to the child, if that aide is required by the child's individualized education program. Tuition received under this paragraph must be used by the academies to provide the required service.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For fiscal year 2008 and later, the special education aid paid to the academies shall be increased by the academy's unreimbursed cost of providing one to one instructional and behavioral management aides assigned to a child, after deducting the special education aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a> attributable to the child, if the aides are required by the child's individualized education program. Aid received under this paragraph must be used by the academies to provide the required service.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For fiscal year 2008 and later, the special education aid paid to the district of the child's residence shall be reduced by the amount paid to the academies for district residents under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.45">127A.45</a>, subdivision 3, beginning in fiscal year 2008, the commissioner shall make an estimated final adjustment payment to the Minnesota State Academies for general education aid and special education aid for the prior fiscal year by August 15.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) For fiscal year 2007, the academies may retain receipts received through mutual agreements with school districts for one to one behavior management aides.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Providing appropriate educational programs.</span></h2>
          <p>When it is determined that the child can benefit from public school enrollment but that the child should also remain in attendance at the applicable school, the district where the institution is located must provide an appropriate educational program for the child and must make a tuition charge to the board of the Minnesota State Academies for the actual cost of providing the program, less any amount of aid received pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.75">125A.75</a>. The board of the Minnesota State Academies must pay the tuition and other program costs including the unreimbursed transportation costs. Aids for children with a disability must be paid to the district providing the special instruction and services. Special transportation must be provided by the district providing the educational program and the state must reimburse that district within the limits provided by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Tuition reduction.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivisions 3 and 5, the board of the Minnesota State Academies may agree to make a tuition charge, or receive an aid adjustment, as applicable, for less than the amount specified in subdivision 3 for pupils attending the applicable school who are residents of the district where the institution is located and who do not board at the institution, if that district agrees to make a tuition charge to the board of the Minnesota State Academies for less than the amount specified in subdivision 5 for providing appropriate educational programs to pupils attending the applicable school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Staff allocation.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivisions 3 and 5, the board of the Minnesota State Academies may agree to supply staff from the Minnesota State Academy for the Deaf and the Minnesota State Academy for the Blind to participate in the programs provided by the district where the institutions are located when the programs are provided to students in attendance at the state schools.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.125A.65.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Student count; tuition.</span></h2>
